So <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10764em;">f</span></span></span></span> crosses zero once on each of the three monotone pieces: three real roots, approximately <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1.5185</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-1.5185</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1.5185</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>0.5085</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">0.5085</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">0.5085</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1.2436</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1.2436</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1.2436</span></span></span></span>, and the other two roots are a complex-conjugate pair.</p>
<p>By the lemma with <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>p</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">p = 5</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.625em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">p</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">5</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Gal</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>f</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>≅</mo><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{Gal}(f) \cong S_5</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mord mathrm">Gal</span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10764em;">f</span><span class="mclose">)</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≅</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.05764em;">S</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0576em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">5</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, which is not solvable. By Galois's theorem, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>f</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">f</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8889em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.10764em;">f</span></span></span></span> is not solvable by radicals. ∎</p>
<blockquote>

Hence it is exactly 6.</p>

mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">9</span></span></span></span>.</p>
</details>
<p><strong>3.</strong> Show that the regular 7-gon is not constructible, using that <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mn>7</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">2\cos(2\pi/7)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mop">cos</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03588em;">π</span><span class="mord">/7</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> is a root of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>+</mo><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>2</mn></msup><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>x</mi><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^3 + x^2 - 2x - 1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8974em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">3</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">+</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8974em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>.</p>
<details>
<summary>Solution</summary>
<p>Rational roots could only be <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>±</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\pm 1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">±</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>, which give <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo>−</mo><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">-1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7278em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord">−</span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span> and <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>1</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">1</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">1</span></span></span></span>, so the cubic has no rational root and is irreducible. Then <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi mathvariant="double-struck">Q</mi><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mn>7</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo>:</mo><mi mathvariant="double-struck">Q</mi><mo stretchy="false">]</mo><mo>=</mo><mn>3</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">[\mathbb{Q}(2\cos(2\pi/7)) : \mathbb{Q}] = 3</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathbb">Q</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.1667em;"></span><span class="mop">cos</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03588em;">π</span><span class="mord">/7</span><span class="mclose">))</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathbb">Q</span><span class="mclose">]</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">3</span></span></span></span>, not a power of 2, so <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>cos</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mn>2</mn><mi>π</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mn>7</mn><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\cos(2\pi/7)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mop">cos</span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord">2</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.03588em;">π</span><span class="mord">/7</span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span> is not constructible, and neither is the 7-gon. (Consistent with Gauss–Wantzel: 7 is prime but not a Fermat prime.)</p>
</details>
<p><strong>4.</strong> List all subfields of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi mathvariant="double-struck">F</mi><mn>64</mn></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbb{F}_{64}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8389em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathbb">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">64</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and draw their inclusions. What is <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="normal">Gal</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><msub><mi mathvariant="double-struck">F</mi><mn>64</mn></msub><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><msub><mi mathvariant="double-struck">F</mi><mn>2</mn></msub><mo stretchy="false">)</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\operatorname{Gal}(\mathbb{F}_{64}/\mathbb{F}_2)</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mop"><span class="mord mathrm">Gal</span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathbb">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight"><span class="mord mtight">64</span></span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathbb">F</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:0em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">2</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mclose">)</span></span></span></span>?</p>
<details>
<summary>Solution</summary>
<p><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mn>64</mn><mo>=</mo><msup><mn>2</mn><mn>6</mn></msup></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">64 = 2^6</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">64</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8141em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord">2</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">6</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> and the divisors of 6 are 1, 2, 3, 6, so the subfields are <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math \mathbb{F}_{64}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8833em;vertical-align:-0.1944em;"></span><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel"><span class="mord vbox"><span is all of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>E</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">E</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6833em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.05764em;">E</span></span></span></span>.</p>
</details>
<p><strong>6.</strong> Explain why every polynomial of degree at most 4 over <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi mathvariant="double-struck">Q</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\mathbb{Q}</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8556em;vertical-align:-0.1667em;"></span><span class="mord mathbb">Q</span></span></span></span> is solvable by radicals, but this argument says nothing about degree 5.</p>
<details>
<summary>Solution</summary>
<p>The Galois group of a degree-<span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span></span></span></span> polynomial is a subgroup of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>n</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_n</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.05764em;">S</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0576em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">n</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>. For <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>≤</mo><mn>4</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n \le 4</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.7719em;vertical-align:-0.136em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">≤</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">4</span></span></span></span>, <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mi>n</mi></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_n</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.05764em;">S</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.1514em;"><span style="top:-2.55em;margin-left:-0.0576em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mathnormal mtight">n</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span> is solvable (chains above), and subgroups of solvable groups are solvable, so Galois's theorem applies. For <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mi>n</mi><mo>=</mo><mn>5</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">n = 5</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.4306em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal">n</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">5</span></span></span></span> the group may be a solvable subgroup of <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>5</mn></msub></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">S_5</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8333em;vertical-align:-0.15em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.05764em;">S</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t vlist-t2"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.3011em;"><span mtight">5</span></span></span></span><span class="vlist-s">​</span></span><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" 2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8974em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mroot><mn>2</mn><mn>5</mn></mroot></mrow><annotation The degree alone does not decide.</p>

<td>normal and separable; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo><mi mathvariant="normal">Aut</mi><mo>⁡</mo><mo stretchy="false">(</mo><mi>E</mi><mi mathvariant="normal">/</mi><mi>F</mi><mo stretchy="false">)</mo><mo stretchy="false">∣</mo><mo>=</mo><mo stretchy="false">[</mo><mi>E</mi><mo>:</mo><mi>F</mi><mo stretchy="false">]</mo></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">\lvert\operatorname{Aut}(E/F)\rvert = [E : F]</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">∣</span><span class="mop"><span class="mord mathrm">Aut</span></span><span class="mopen">(</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.05764em;">E</span><span class="mord">/</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.13889em;">F</span><span class="mclose">)∣</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">=</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mopen">[</span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.05764em;">E</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span><span class="mrel">:</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2778em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:1em;vertical-align:-0.25em;"></span><span class="mord mathnormal" style="margin-right:0.13889em;">F</span><span class="mclose">]</span></span></span></span></td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td>Fundamental Theorem</td>
<td>subgroups ↔ intermediate fields, order-reversing; normal ↔ Galois</td>
</tr>
<tr>
<td><span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msup><mi>x</mi><mn>3</mn></msup><mo>−</mo><mn>2</mn></mrow><annotation encoding="application/x-tex">x^3 - 2</annotation></semantics></math></span><span class="katex-html" aria-hidden="true"><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.8974em;vertical-align:-0.0833em;"></span><span class="mord"><span class="mord mathnormal">x</span><span class="msupsub"><span class="vlist-t"><span class="vlist-r"><span class="vlist" style="height:0.8141em;"><span style="top:-3.063em;margin-right:0.05em;"><span class="pstrut" style="height:2.7em;"></span><span class="sizing reset-size6 size3 mtight"><span class="mord mtight">3</span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span><span class="mbin">−</span><span class="mspace" style="margin-right:0.2222em;"></span></span><span class="base"><span class="strut" style="height:0.6444em;"></span><span class="mord">2</span></span></span></span></td>
<td>splitting field degree 6, group <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML"><semantics><mrow><msub><mi>S</mi><mn>3</mn></msub></mrow><annotation class="vlist" style="height:0.15em;"><span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span></span>, subfield <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math </tr>
<tr>
<td>Solvable group</td>
<td>chain of normal steps with abelian quotients; <span class="katex"><span class="katex-mathml"><math <tr>
<td>Galois's criterion</td>
<td>solvable by radicals ⇔ solvable Galois group (char 0)</td>
</tr>

<h1 id="Whats-next"><a href="#Whats-next" class="headerlink" title="What's next"></a>What's next</h1>
<p>This post completes the core course: from the integers and modular arithmetic (post 00), through groups, actions and Sylow theory (posts 01–03), rings and fields (post 04), to Galois theory. The table below lists the planned extensions. Each builds directly on the material here, so the series can grow without renumbering the existing posts.</p>

<h1 id="References"><a href="#References" class="headerlink" title="References"></a>References</h1>
<ul>
<li>D. S. Dummit and R. M. Foote, <em>Abstract Algebra</em>, 3rd edition, Wiley, 2004 — Chapters 13 (field extensions, constructions, splitting fields, finite fields) and 14 (Galois theory, finite fields, Galois groups of polynomials, solvability by radicals).</li>
<li>M. Artin, <em>Algebra</em>, 2nd edition, Pearson, 2011 — Chapters 15 (fields, ruler and compass) and 16 (Galois theory, quintic equations).</li>
<li>I. Stewart, <em>Galois Theory</em>, 4th edition, CRC Press, 2015 — a readable, historically informed development, including Abel–Ruffini.</li>
<li>J. S. Milne, <a href="https://www.jmilne.org/math/CourseNotes/" target="_blank" rel="noopener external nofollow noreferrer"><em>Fields and Galois Theory</em></a>, course notes (free).</li>
<li>E. Artin, <em>Galois Theory</em>, Notre Dame Mathematical Lectures 2, 1942 (Dover reprint) — the classic linear-algebra approach to the Fundamental Theorem.</li>
<li>MIT OpenCourseWare, <a href="https://ocw.mit.edu/courses/18-702-algebra-ii-spring-2011/" target="_blank" rel="noopener external nofollow noreferrer">18.702 Algebra II (Spring 2011)</a> — lecture notes on fields and Galois theory.</li>
<li>K. Conrad, <a href="https://kconrad.math.uconn.edu/blurbs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener external nofollow noreferrer">Expository papers (&quot;blurbs&quot;)</a> — short notes on Galois theory, including Galois groups of cubics and quartics.</li>
</ul>
